# FireSim — Project Record

Living record of the work, decisions, references and testing behind **FireSim**, the
Canadian FBP wildfire spread simulator for municipal EOC planning. This file in the repo
(`docs/PROJECT_RECORD.md`) is the source of truth; copies attached elsewhere are snapshots.
Append to it; don't rewrite history. Compiled 2026-10-08 from the repository, its docs, the
git history and the owner's recorded decisions.

- **Live:** https://wildfire-simulator.vercel.app (frontend, Vercel) · API https://firesim-api.fly.dev/api/v1/health (Fly.io `firesim-api`, region yyz)
- **Repo:** https://github.com/Tphambolio/wildfire-simulator (local: `~/dev/wildfire/wildfire-simulator-v3`), branch `master`
- **Owner:** Travis Kennedy, P.Ag (City of Edmonton)
- **Tracked in Claude for Science project** `proj_4c36553a0c0c` (snapshots of this record are attached there; this file stays the source of truth)
- **Status at this revision (v7 branch `feat/long-range-embers-v7`, 2026-10-17):** long-range embers v7 (calibrated front, then embers) pre-registered (`PREREG_LR7`, `a5fa8ba`; addenda `c77a96b`, `a9fc0b5`) and scored on fresh held-out Dixie and Caldor: target A FAIL (calibrated front not better than v6's, −0.44 h, CI −1.60 to +0.47; embers worse, MAE +0.31 h, Brier +0.187); target B1 met (+0.067 AUC vs density + year built, year built absent on the fresh fires) but embers add nothing (B2) and AUC 0.64 < 0.70 [R48]. Previous revision (v6, 2026-10-15): long-range embers v6 reframed validation pre-registered (`PREREG_LR6`, `83e8c9f`) and scored: target A (community arrival) FAIL, target B (combined building-loss model) met but without ember contribution, AUC 0.60 [R47]. Earlier status: branch `feat/long-range-embers` (PR #59, held): **long-range embers v3** (opt-in, illustrative; spec §6.5; API `structure_long_range_model: "v3"`): v2 embers + probabilistic building-to-building links (exp(−separation/λ) to 50 m), building survival s, ember factor k, wind-event end of the run; λ 160 m, s 0.3, k 100 fitted on Eaton/Palisades/Camp (leave-one-fire-out Brier skill vs base rate negative on every fold; Platt and exposure-weighted recalibration also negative). **Pre-registered held-out test (`PREREG_LR3`, `adcee43`) on Tubbs 2017 + Woolsey 2018: FAIL** (1 of 4 conditions: κ vs windward seeding +0.036, +0.014 to +0.060; Brier vs Chen & McAneney −0.040, −0.059 to −0.019; κ vs distance band +0.004, −0.011 to +0.020; Brier vs base rate −0.254, −0.269 to −0.240; pooled AUC 0.50) [R37].
- **Previous status (v2):** branch `feat/long-range-embers` (PR #59, held; merged with `master` `a1ff495`, PRs #62-#67): **long-range embers v2** (opt-in, illustrative; spec §6.4, C28-C31; API `structure_long_range_model` "v2" default / "v1"): burning buildings are ember sources too (Qin GR' 10 per MJ, Himoto & Tanaka transport, 10 min coupling with an incremental Hamada stage: wildland → town → town); ignition through one 1.44 m² receptive bed per building with the per-brand probability from firebrand-shower tests (p_bed per building, mass factor 4, p_b U(0.1, 1) per member) instead of v1's saturating match-flame chain; yield and p_b drawn per member. Pre-registered (`PREREG_LR2`, `c6399f7`, pushed before scoring) on **Palisades 2025 (independent, data prepared blind): not worth showing** — κ beats windward seeding (+0.175, +0.130 to +0.224) and the distance band (+0.096, +0.040 to +0.160) but Brier vs Chen & McAneney +0.061 (−0.011 to +0.131) includes zero; AUC 0.50, BSS vs climatology −0.57, P ≥ 0.9 → 60 % destroyed; ~1,620 involved among 5,370 unsurveyed buildings within 1 km outside. Eaton/Camp disclosed checks: Eaton Brier better than v1, κ still below (b)/(d); Camp worse [R33]; report ids of this branch renumbered to [R31]/[R32] after the merge; tests §5.
- **Previous status (v1 of this branch):** branch `feat/long-range-embers` (from `master` `1b377b9`, PRs #55-#57 merged; merged with `master` `a0690d8`, PR #54): opt-in **long-range ember ignition** (spec §6.3, C25-C27; API `structure_long_range_embers`, `_members`): literature-only chain (500 embers/kg from torching/crowning cells, untruncated Sardoy landing capped at the Albini maximum, per-building P = 1 − exp(−Σ λ A_r p_e k_g p_b), ensemble over the structure stage); the map fades buildings by probability (illustrative, map only). Pre-registered (`PREREG_LR`, `2b67725`, pushed before scoring): **Eaton 2025 not worth showing** (BSS vs Chen & McAneney +0.21 but κ 0.115 < windward-edge 0.162 and distance band 0.131; poorly calibrated, BSS vs climatology −0.53); Camp 2018 worse (AUC 0.38); Jasper not run [R31], [R32]; tests: changed files only locally (§5).

---

## 1. Purpose and audience

FireSim simulates wildfire growth and fire behaviour with the Canadian Forest Fire Behavior
Prediction (FBP) System and reports where and when the modelled fire reaches neighbourhoods,
critical assets, roads and buildings.

- **Primary user:** the EOC **Planning section** (Situation Unit, Plans Chief), plus emergency
  management and fire officers trained in the FBP System. The main screen is built around
  **resource deployment**: head / flank / back, head fire intensity class and what it means for
  suppression, clock-time arrivals.
- **Intended use now:** preparedness, training, tabletop exercises and what-if planning.
- **Later goal:** operational fire-growth prediction during incidents with Prometheus/WISE-level
  accuracy in real time, once validation on observed fires supports it. Not there yet (§4.4).
- **What it is not:** an evacuation decision tool (it never suggests Order / Alert / Watch; the
  Planning section sets them), a structure-loss or ignition-probability model, a source of
  firefighter, aircraft or RPAS safety distances, or a replacement for the lead agency's
  prediction.
- **Disclaimers the product must carry:** "planning tool" limits badge; outputs shared beyond
  Planning state issuer, run time, model version (`/api/v1/version` git SHA) and inputs; a single
  run is not the expected fire (`docs/model-card.md`, "Responsible use").

## 2. Architecture

Frontend → HTTP/WebSocket → API → engine; frames are streamed back over WebSocket
(`docs/architecture.md`). The engine is pure Python with no web dependencies.

| File / module | Role |
|---|---|
| `engine/src/firesim/fbp/` (`calculator.py`, `constants.py`, `crown_fire.py`) | FBP System, 18 fuel types (ST-X-3 + GLC-X-10), verified against `cffdrs`; vectorised path `fbp_ellipse_arrays` |
| `engine/src/firesim/fwi/` (`calculator.py`, `classes.py`) | FWI System (Van Wagner & Pickett 1985), hourly FFMC; CWFIS FWI map classes |
| `engine/src/firesim/spread/cellular.py` | Level-set grid model (ELMFIRE-style Huygens velocity from each cell's FBP ellipse); per-cell arrival, speed, head/flank/back; active edges; flame panels |
| `engine/src/firesim/spread/huygens.py`, `ellipse.py`, `slope.py` | Huygens wavelets on uniform fuel; shared FBP layer `fbp_for_conditions`; ellipse geometry; slope factor |
| `engine/src/firesim/spread/diurnal.py` | Opt-in burning period and hourly-FFMC spin-up from 17:00 local |
| `engine/src/firesim/spread/ensemble.py`, `montecarlo.py` | Ensemble P10/P50/P90 arrival + burn probability; simple Monte Carlo burn probability |
| `engine/src/firesim/spread/albini.py`, `spotting.py` | Albini/Chase/Morris maximum spotting distance; heuristic emission/landing |
| `engine/src/firesim/spread/simulator.py` | `Simulator` orchestrator; picks grid model whenever a fuel grid exists |
| `engine/src/firesim/exposure.py` | Building exposure: distance bands, Cohen (2004) radiant flux, flux-time index |
| `engine/src/firesim/structures/` | Structure-to-structure spread (opt-in, illustrative; `embers.py` since 2026-10-10: opt-in ember ignition, design fire, Himoto/Sardoy transport, ψ criterion, spec §6.1): `units.py` builds one unit per building footprint (centroid, footprint in local metres, area, square-equivalent size, neighbour graph with edge-to-edge separations within a cutoff, default 30 m); `hamada.py` Hamada rates; `spread.py` front contact (from the building's grid cells) + building-to-building Dijkstra (API `structure_spread`, default off), units built only for the area the spread can reach (`structure_spread_for_grid_run`, footprint source = `BuildingIndex` or a list; memory guard 60,000 units), involved-unit detail for the map; specification `docs/structure-spread-spec.md` |
| `engine/src/firesim/structures/long_range.py` | Long-range ember ignition (opt-in `structure_long_range_embers`, 2026-10-11; illustrative): generation from torching/crowning cells, untruncated Sardoy landing field capped at the Albini maximum (FFT per time bin × class), per-building Poisson ignition hazard, ensemble over the structure stage → per-building probability (spec §6.3) [R31], [R32] |
| `engine/src/firesim/data/` | Fuel loader (code schemes incl. Edmonton canopy LiDAR, drone pipeline, CFS national), DEM loader, reprojection (`raster_grid.py`), WUI/water/building masks, synthetic demo grid |
| `engine/src/firesim/validation/` | CFSDS validation harness (`cfsds.py`, `harness.py`, `metrics.py`, `report.py`, `weather.py`) |
| `api/src/firesim_api/` | FastAPI: `routers/` (simulations, fwi, weather, health), `services/runner.py` (background runs, grid cache), `schemas/`, `ws/` |
| `frontend/src/` | React 19 + TypeScript + Vite + MapLibre GL: `MapView`, Setup sections, Situation panel (`FireMetrics` run details, `EvacStatusPanel`, `CriticalAssetsPanel`, `EOCSummary` actions), `TimeSlider`, `EOCConsole` (ICS forms, ICS Canada 209-WF, full EOC summary), `AboutPanel` (About & sources tab); shared tables `utils/fireClasses.ts`, `utils/fwiClass.ts`, `utils/time.ts` |
| `frontend/src/components/StructureSpreadPanel.tsx`, `InfoTip.tsx`, `utils/structureSpread.ts` | House-to-house spread in the app: Situation card (counts, stacked chart with the timeline cursor, map toggle), map layer `struct-units-*` in `MapView.tsx` (involved footprints by mechanism, legend, hover/click popup), caveat in a keyboard-accessible tooltip |
| `frontend/src/components/InfoTip.tsx`, `Badge.tsx`, `FwiClassChip.tsx`, `HfiClassChip.tsx`, `content/explanations.ts`, `styles/infotip.css` | Accessible explanation pop-ups (toggletip; `TipCore`, `InfoTip`, `TipButton`, `Badge`) and the single source of all tip/badge text (shared with the About tab and tests) |
| `frontend/src/components/AboutPanel.tsx`, `styles/about.css` | "About & sources" tab: intended use, limits and validation, methods and references, data and licences, responsible use, ICS Canada forms, help, version |
| `frontend/src/components/RunProgress.tsx`, `hooks/useSimulation.ts`; `api/.../services/runner.py` (`SimulationRun.set_phase`, `RunCancelled`), `routers/simulations.py` (`simulation.status`); engine `Simulator(progress=)`, `run_cellular_simulation(progress=)` | Main-run progress (phases, spread fraction, cancel mid-computation); run tokens so a new run ignores the old socket and polls; `clearResults` |
| `frontend/src/utils/scenarioDefaults.ts` | Scenario fallback config, matching the Setup defaults (WUI modifiers and OSM water off) |
| `scripts/build_edmonton_assets.py` | Builds `frontend/public/edmonton/assets.geojson` (critical assets) from open sources |
| `scripts/build_edmonton_roads.py` | Builds `frontend/public/edmonton/roads.geojson` (motorway to secondary + ramps) from Overpass |
| `scripts/structure_sensitivity.py` | Structure-spread sensitivity (cutoff, contact distance, footprint size, front-contact rule) on Edmonton footprints; aggregate outputs written outside the repo [R7], [R8] |
| `scripts/jasper_structure_validation.py` | (`--embers`: the pre-registered ember-ignition check, `PREREG_EMBERS` [R16]) Pre-registered end-state check of the Hamada option on the Jasper 2024 townsite losses (structure-only, ember entry imposed). Downloads the Municipality of Jasper damage layer with `OBJECTID` and `Status` only; the cache and outputs live outside the repo (`~/dev/wildfire/validation-data/jasper2024/`) and are never committed [R10] |
| `scripts/build_building_vegetation.py` | Structure step 3 data (offline, open data only): `canopy-raster` (open-ground canopy share on ≈ 5 m cells from the Meta 1 m CHM → `data/edmonton_canopy_5m.tif`), `ring-metrics` (per-footprint canopy rings, overhang, stand distance for any area), `edmonton-table` (→ `data/edmonton_building_vegetation.csv.gz`) [R21], [R23] |
| `scripts/structure_step3_validation.py` | Pre-registered step-3 tests (`PREREG_STEP3`, `PREREG_STEP3_EATON`, `PREREG_STEP3_CAMP`): vegetation-bridged cutoff and roof scenario vs Hamada, distance band and distance + vegetation baselines; `independent --fire eaton|camp` (CAL FIRE DINS), `jasper` (non-independent check), `--dry-run` (model only, random outcomes) [R23] |
| `scripts/long_range_ember_validation.py` | Pre-registered long-range ember test (`PREREG_LR`, `2b67725`): imposed wildland source from the FRAP perimeter, documented progression rule and FBP proxy fuel; Eaton primary, Camp secondary; Brier/BSS, log score, AUC, reliability, κ; baselines a-e; 250 m block bootstrap; `--dry-run` never reads outcomes [R32] |
| `scripts/long_range_v2_validation.py`, `scripts/long_range_v2_figures.py` | Pre-registered v2 test (`PREREG_LR2`, `c6399f7`): Palisades 2025 primary, Eaton/Camp disclosed checks; 1 km outside-ring buildings simulated but not scored (false alarms); count-matched ties by seeded permutation; figures (chain diagram, block maps, reliability, comparisons, loss vs distance) [R33] |
| `scripts/long_range_v3_validation.py`, `long_range_v3_recalibrate.py`, `long_range_v3_figures.py` | v3 calibration (grid + leave-one-fire-out on Eaton/Palisades/Camp), recalibration CV, pre-registered held-out test `PREREG_LR3` (`adcee43`) on Tubbs/Woolsey, figures and small summaries [R37] |
| `scripts/fuelgrid/` | Open-data FBP fuel-grid pipeline (fetch → Edmonton LiDAR labels → build → validate → engine check; `python -m scripts.fuelgrid.run all`): 20 m EPSG:3776 leaf-on / leaf-off grids, percent conifer and a per-cell rule band for Edmonton and St. Albert; downloads and outputs in `$FUELGRID_DATA` (outside the repo) [R17], `docs/fuel-grid-open-data.md`. **v2** (`key2.py`, `sources2.py`, `build2.py`, `validate2.py`; `run all2` / `firesim2`): full FBP key + disturbance, stand-level typing, northern Alberta test areas (EPSG:3400) [R26] |
| `scripts/validate.py`, `scripts/validation/` | Validation runs (prepare / run / report / compare), CFSDS fetch, optional WISE fire-day runs |

**External data the code calls at run time:** Open-Meteo forecast API (`frontend/src/services/api.ts`,
`api/.../routers/weather.py`); CWFIS GeoServer WFS (`routers/weather.py`, current station
weather/FWI); OSM raster tiles and OpenTopoMap tiles, optional Mapbox satellite (`MapView.tsx`);
Nominatim geocoding and Overpass (`WeatherPanel.tsx`, `services/overpass.ts`). Build/validation
time only: City of Edmonton Open Data (Socrata, incl. Parcel Addresses `ut27-nrpn`), Government
of Alberta continuing care list (open.alberta.ca), StatCan ODHF, Overpass
(`build_edmonton_assets.py`, `build_edmonton_roads.py`); Open-Meteo ERA5 archive (`validation/weather.py`); CFSDS (OSF),
CFS national FBP grids, MRDEM (`scripts/validation/`).

**Run storage (2026-10-11, [R30]):** runs live in API memory (≤ `FIRESIM_MAX_STORED_RUNS`, 40) and, once finished, in the run archive (`api/.../services/archive.py`, `docs/run-archive.md`): a SQLite index (`FIRESIM_ARCHIVE_DB`, WAL; one row per run: status, times, request, git SHA, fuel/buildings source, area, duration, frames) and payloads in an object store (`local` default or `s3` = Cloudflare R2: `<id>/response.json.gz` = the live GET body, `arrival.json.gz`, `ensemble.json.gz`, `meta.json`). Reads: memory, then archive. Retention: 365 days, optional byte cap, at startup and every 24 h. Index backed up by Litestream to R2 when the secrets exist (`api/deploy/entrypoint.sh`). Without a volume or settings: a temporary directory with a startup warning (`/api/v1/health` `archive.durable: false`). Incidents, periods and annotations stay in browser `localStorage` (phase 2). Stored: model output and requests only; per-building fields are those the live GET already returns (map display only, never exported).

**Build / deploy:** push to `master` → GitHub Actions (`engine-tests (3.11)`, `engine-tests (3.12)`,
`frontend` required; `frontend-e2e` not required) → `deploy` job runs
`flyctl deploy --remote-only --build-arg GIT_SHA=…` (API image bundles `data/` at `/app/data`);
Vercel rebuilds the frontend from `master` (`docs/deployment.md`).

Units are the 1,119 MOJ townsite footprints; Destroyed = positive (Destroyed + Visible Damage as a sensitivity).<br>• **Ember entry:** 15 seed buildings (the 5 nearest each of the three after-action-review first-impact areas) ignited at 18:00 on 24 July 2024.<br>• **Hamada:** engine defaults, 30 m cutoff and f_b = 1; 10 m wind 15 km/h from 225° (NOR-X-433 pp.33-36); window 18:00-24:00.<br>• **Sensitivity:** cutoff 20 / 45 m; wind × 0.75 / × 1.25; wind from 202.5°; window 4 h / 12 h; four alternative seed sets.<br>• **Baselines:** count-matched distance band; FPI < 5 m one-step and percolation rules; random at the observed rate; seeds only.<br>• **Decision rule:** structure spread is worth showing only if its κ beats the distance band and seeds only (spec §8 item 4) | Spec §8 item 5: no tuning on the test fire. | 2026-10-10 | **Ensemble empty-member fix and partial-failure rule.** Production (Fly, simulation 70fa2753, Edmonton 4 h grid run): "The ensemble did not finish (index 0 is out of bounds for axis 0 with size 0)". Root cause: the ignition hold in `run_cellular_simulation` accepted a head ROS > 1e-6 m/min, the starting ellipse (`_initial_front`) required > 1e-5; a rate between them gave a starting fire with no burned cell and `_window` indexed an empty mask. How members got there: FBP (cffdrs) floors a zero ROS at 1e-6 ("no spread", e.g. D-2 green aspen below BUI 80); the member's DMC/DC perturbation took BUI below 80 at a D-2 ignition and its ROS multiplier (log-normal, median 1) scaled the floor to 1e-6..1e-5. Members with multiplier ≤ 1 held forever (0 cells, the logged "0 cells burned"), > 1 crashed, > 10 burned the ignition cell spuriously. The deterministic run (BUI above 80, multiplier 1) spread. Evidence: D-2 grid, BUI 87, 30 members seed 1, old engine 7 zero / 6 IndexError / 17 burning, each crash exactly a BUI < 80 member with multiplier > 1; the same members on the Edmonton grid (ignition in a D-2 stand) reproduce the production error via the API. Fix: one threshold `CARRY_ROS` = 1e-5 for hold and ellipse; FBP's floored ROS is set to 0 before any multiplier (ensemble ROS error or WUI modifier), as it means no spread, not a rate; `_window` returns empty slices for an empty mask and a starting fire without a cell is treated as no fire (a zero-burn run returns frames with 0 ha). Ensemble: each member runs in a try/except (logged with member index, seed and perturbation); percentiles, burn probability and area range are over the finished members, **zero-burn members included** (they are in the burn-probability denominator); the ensemble fails only when **fewer than 50 %** of members finish (`MIN_OK_FRACTION`; below that the statistics would rest on few, possibly unrepresentative members, since failures depend on the perturbation). API adds `members_ok`, `members_failed`, `failed`; the card shows "k of N members failed and are left out". Not fixed (open item §6, seasonal fuel switch): the Edmonton LiDAR grid labels aspen D-2 year-round, so in leaf-off months the D-2 BUI-80 switch applies where D-1 would spread | Owner bug report 2026-10-10; `engine/tests/spread/test_ensemble_robustness.py`, API `test_ensemble_*` |
| 2026-10-10 | **M1** Grass curing default: date-aware. 95 % in the pre-green-up window (day of year 60-149, about 1 March to 29 May) [H for the dates and the value]; outside it no silent default: the API returns 422 when O-1 grass can burn and no `grass_cure` is given, the UI shows the field as required and suggests the last value entered. Explicit values are always used (backward compatible for clients that send one) | Owner delegated the mechanics choices on 2026-10-10 ("go with your views on the mechanics"); decisions report [R12], evidence [R13] rec 1. The fixed 60 % gives curing factor 0.20 (GLC-X-10 eq 35b, p.9); GLC-X-26 uses 90 % when curing is not observed (p.24); grass before green-up is cured (Pickell et al. 2017; Beverly & Schroeder 2025 p.14; NRC 2021 p.25). Implemented and validated on CFSDS in PR `feat/curing-default-and-citations` [R14] (§4.4) |
| 2026-10-10 | **M2** LiDAR crown base height stays out of FBP runs; a separate Perrakis et al. (2023) crown-fire likelihood layer later | [R12], [R13] rec 2: FBP CBH values were tuned, not measured (ST-X-3 p.35); GLC-X-10 p.30 advises against changing CBH outside C-6. Planned (§6) |
| 2026-10-10 | **M3** Area over-prediction is fixed through run set-up (active edges, burning period, spin-up); any spread-rate correction only by fire type and fuel, never a blanket ROS cut | [R12], [R13] rec 3: FBP under-predicts spread in most published comparisons (Cruz & Alexander 2013 p.16; Stocks et al. 2004 p.1557; Beverly & Schroeder 2025 p.8), FireSim's largest runs too; the over-prediction is set-up (Bennett et al. 2026 p.9). Open item reworded (§6) |
| 2026-10-10 | **M4** Embers from torching/crowning cells (Van Wagner criteria) instead of HFI ≥ 4,000 kW/m; add Albini, Alexander & Cruz (2012) active crown-fire spotting; LiDAR tree heights as Albini inputs | [R12], [R13] rec 4: Albini (1979, pp.3-5) is a torching-tree model guided by Van Wagner's criteria. Planned (§6) |
| 2026-10-10 | **M5** Native 20 m fuel grid in a window around buildings, 50 m elsewhere; where several fuel maps are used, average predictions, never fuels; memory budget checked against the 2 GB server first | [R12], [R13] rec 5: Forbes & Beverly (2024); Bennett, Da Silva & Boisvert (2024, p.11). Planned (§6) |
| 2026-10-10 | **M5 implemented: 20 m WUI window and majority-class coarsening.** (1) `load_fuel_grid` coarsens categorical rasters with the majority (GDAL mode) class, nearest neighbour only at or below the source size (`raster_grid.categorical_resampling`). (2) A single-day grid run whose window (50 m burned box + max(500 m, half its side)) holds buildings is repeated at the raster's native cell size (20 m) on a crop of that window (`spread/wui_window.py`, `data/fine_grid.py`): same level set, weather, seed and building mask; kept only if no burned cell is within 6 cells of an inner crop edge and no spot lands off the crop (margin doubled, 3 attempts); guards 600,000 crop cells and 60,000 burned cells, else the 50 m run with the reason. Frames carry `grid`; structure units over the whole run grid (`area_bbox`), front contact on the crop. Not for multi-day continuations, perimeter override or ensembles (50 m). `FIRESIM_WUI_FINE_GRID=0` disables; no new request/UI option | A nested mixed-resolution level set needs two-way coupling and mixed-resolution outputs for every consumer; a crop re-run keeps one grid per run and the verified level set unchanged. Nearest neighbour is a point sample (one 20 m cell in 6.25 kept); the mode is the defensible aggregate but halves C-2 patches and, like nearest, loses sub-cell breaks (only the 20 m window keeps them). Guard sized so the crop never needs much more memory than the 50 m whole-city run (265 B/cell) [R18] |
| 2026-10-10 | **M6** City tree inventory as an ember-source layer (364 managed conifer clusters outside the uPLVI boundary, treated as torching trees, not stand crown fire) only if its licence allows | [R12], [R13] rec 9. Planned, licence check first (§6) |
| 2026-10-10 | **Drought Code on cold days ([R15] M1):** at T ≤ −2.8 °C the temperature is floored at −2.8, so the day-length term Lf still counts (V = Lf; a negative V adds nothing); previously FireSim added nothing (−0.45 to −3.2 DC per cold day, April-October) | Owner approved fix 1 of [R15]. Van Wagner (1987) eqs 25-26, FTR-35 printed p. 14 (PDF p. 25), Table 3 printed p. 15, give V = 0.36(T + 2.8) + Lf with no cut-off; the −2.8 floor is the Van Wagner & Pickett (1985) program rule as carried by cffdrs (R `drought_code.r` l. 39; Python `fwi.drought_code`). FTR-33 is not on disk, so the FORTRAN line itself is unverified. `b2cf6f9` |

| 2026-10-10 | **Starting FWI codes come from Pyra's chain** (owner goal: "the fire-weather product and the prediction start from the same numbers"). `/api/v1/weather/current` asks a "pyra" tier first: for a point in Alberta it takes the nearest station in Pyra's list and reproduces Pyra's station page (`initFWI`, Pyra `38aee4f`): carry-over = the newer of the CWFIS current-layer pick (Pyra's holding cache) and Pyra's published `data/cwfis_prev.json` entry, ≤ 2 days old; before noon LST stepped one day with the GEM noon-LST forecast (the same Open-Meteo URL Pyra requests); after noon CWFIS codes as published, else stepped with a weather-only CWFIS observation or GEM noon. FireSim's own calculator does the step (same equations, 147.2). Labels follow Pyra ("Noon LST Forecast · today (pre-noon)"). Pyra's rules are copied as they are, including the ones this record would not choose (one step from a 2-day-old carry-over, [R15] M13; the spring DC floor heuristic; a co-located station's codes when the CWFIS order puts it first), because the point is the same number, not a better one; differences are listed in §6 for the Pyra session. Fallback: CWFIS → archive → GEM cold start (labelled), with the reason the Pyra tier was not used appended. User-entered codes still win. `FIRESIM_PYRA_SOURCE=0` turns the tier off | Why consume Pyra's inputs rather than its numbers: Pyra publishes no "today" codes; they are computed in the browser from the carry-over file and a live GEM request, so FireSim must repeat that step. Why server-side and cached: one cached fetch of a ~155 KB file per 15 min (raw.githubusercontent.com CDN ~5 min, file changes once a day at 20:15 UTC); the last good copy is used on failure up to Pyra's 48 h limit, then GitHub Pages, then the old chain. Why a copy of Pyra's station list (`services/pyra_stations.py`, exported by `scripts/pyra/export_pyra_stations.mjs`): Pyra publishes it only inside `fwi.js`; a check against a Pyra checkout runs when `PYRA_DIR` is set. `source_tier` (not `source`) carries the machine-readable "pyra" because `source` is already the display string the UI shows. Parity: §5 |
| 2026-10-10 | **Building burn-out (structure spread step 4; spec §4.3, C21).** Every involved unit burns the building design fire and is burnt out when it ends: 66 min (150 kW/m², PROCI24 p.3) or 70 min (400 kW/m², FSJ104686 p.2) after involvement; the end of the curve is the end of combustion (PROCI24 p.2; Qin25 eq 6.2 p.146). A burnt-out unit passes no fire: a Hamada crossing not complete by then never arrives [H] and ember emission stops (as before, Qin25 p.67). API `structure_burnout` default **on** (false = published Hamada, which has no burnout, FSJ104651 p.3); one design fire per run (`structure_design_fire_kw_m2`) sets both stages. Not adopted: Lee & Davidson's fully-developed-phase-only spread (p.673; a 1 min window with the PROCI24 curve). Default chosen on consistency grounds, before and independent of the Jasper result | [R19]; commits `5753b8a`, `82cc161`, `e4b2bed` |

| 2026-10-13 | **Decision (owner, Travis Kennedy, EOC Chief of Plans): ember validation is split in two (long-range embers v6).** (A) *Ember transport and community arrival*: long-range embers are scored on spot-fire distance distributions (Storey et al. 2020 *Fire* 3:10; Cruz et al. 2012 for long-range maxima), on the timing of fire arrival at communities (NIST TN 2135 Camp timeline, GOFER timed perimeters, VIIRS detection times) and on ignitions ahead of the front, against FireSim without long-range embers. (B) *Building loss with a combined model*: ember arrival starts it, house-to-house spread (building density, age/code, near-building vegetation) decides which buildings are lost, scored against DINS with density-only and density+age baselines; realistic AUC 0.7-0.8 checked against Zamanialaei et al. 2025 Tables 1-2 p.7 (partly verified, spec C41). Why: the v5 diagnosis on the training fires (v3 saturates; wildland ember load and edge distance rank destruction backwards, AUC 0.31-0.39; density within 100 m 0.68-0.73; year built 0.57-0.66; Eaton ≈ 0.5; Woolsey reversed, likely the unsurveyed-as-undamaged rule). Owner standing rule: long-range embers is a core principle, push hard, never park. Pre-registration `PREREG_LR6` (`83e8c9f`, sha256 `ad293486…dfdf`), spec §6.8. | spec §6.8, `docs/PREREG_LR6.md` |
| 2026-10-15 | **Result: long-range embers v6 (PREREG_LR6, `83e8c9f`).** **Target A FAIL**: on held-out Glass, CZU and North Complex (279 community blocks of 540 m) long-range ember spot fires (η 1e-8, fitted on training fires; objective J = mean of MAE and Brier ratios vs A0, changed from MAE alone before scoring because one-sided VIIRS bounds never penalise early arrival) made community-arrival error worse: MAE 11.17 vs 10.64 h, +0.53 h (95 % +0.34 to +0.75); Brier of P(reached by hour) +0.021 (+0.015 to +0.027). Cause: FireSim's front with proxy C-4/C-7 fuels is ~10 h wrong (Glass/North Complex 10-15 h early, CZU late); embers only add spread, so help where the front is slow (Camp training 4.62 → 3.51 h at η 1e-5; CZU at η 1e-7 9.06 → 8.45 h) and hurt where it is fast. Spot-distance tail P(>3 km)/P(>1 km) 0.106 vs Storey 0.20 (pre-registered band 0.10-0.40 met), maximum 10.7 km; FireSim's short-range spotting has no tail (max 2.7 km). **Target B**: combined model (density, year built, separation, canopy 0-30 m, fire reach, landed embers) vs density+age: fire-averaged held-out AUC 0.604 vs 0.507, +0.096 (+0.068 to +0.125) — **met** — but embers add nothing (+0.0001) and AUC is below the 0.70 reference; density alone reverses (0.449). Woolsey labels fixed (surveyed only). Embers stay opt-in, illustrative; next: fix the front (real fuels, hourly winds, spread calibration) and re-test embers on it [R47]. | [R47], `docs/PREREG_LR6.md` |

| 2026-10-17 | **Bug: long-range ember kernels allocated gigabytes and ran for hours at extreme intensity.** At the highest fireline intensities the v5 Sardoy short-range lognormal has medians of hundreds to hundreds of thousands of km (a 2.6e9 m cap on Caldor). The landing kernel was built over its full box: a v7 Tubbs stage-2 run reached 14.6-16 GB and crashed the workstation (06:33), held-out runs stalled or were killed at 5 GB. Fix (`a5fa8ba`, `c77a96b`, `a9fc0b5`): kernels cropped to the offsets a same-size convolution on the grid uses; full-kernel normalising sum over the crosswind strip where the normal factor is non-zero, analytic lognormal mass beyond 100,000 cells, column chunks for east/west strips. Kept cells bit-identical to `72b0a76` (300 random kernels); 5 training re-runs bit-identical in arrival and spots (landed ≤ 4.6e-13); tests in `engine/tests/structures/test_long_range.py`. The Sardoy extrapolation itself is unchanged (an open item). | `a5fa8ba`, `c77a96b`, `a9fc0b5` |
| 2026-10-13 | **Bug: spot fires from the grid model's ember spotting never spread when they landed outside the burned area's box.** Found while building long-range embers v6 (target A, spec §6.8). `run_cellular_simulation(enable_spotting=True)` set a spot cell's phi to −h/2 and its arrival time, but on the next step a cell outside the previous computational window (the bounding box of burned cells + 6 cells) counts as "entering" and its phi is reset from the distance to the burned area, which put the spot cell back to +h/2: the spot was recorded (frame `spot_fires`, arrival time) but did not spread. Spots inside the box were unaffected. Synthetic check (C-4, 90 m, 40 km/h, 4 h): burned cells 6,529 without spotting, 6,474 with spotting before the fix (268 spots recorded), 13,271 after. Fix: `_seed_spot` sets phi to the signed distance from the spot in a band of 6 cells and marks the band (`engine/src/firesim/spread/cellular.py`); regression test `engine/tests/spread/test_spot_seeding.py`. Effect: opt-in spotting runs (API `enable_spotting`, the app's spotting option) now grow spot fires; results of earlier spotting runs under-stated spotting. The v6 target-A baseline (front + short-range spotting) uses the fixed behaviour. | `64e6ac7` |
| 2026-10-11 | **Bug: re-running showed the old run (single with ensemble → multi-day).** Owner: after a quick single run with the ensemble on, switching Setup → Run options to Multi-day and running again still showed the old 4 h run while the app polled `GET /simulations/db74ce05/ensemble` every ~2 s for minutes; clearing first made the multi-day run work. Investigation: the code on `8743ad5` (= the production deployment) resets the frames and the ensemble key on every start, and a Playwright reproduction of exactly that flow against the mocked API passed on `8743ad5` (multi-day request sent, old poll stopped, 72 h timeline) — **the stale view did not reproduce**. What the reproduction cannot show is the server side: the old run's ensemble kept computing in the API process (it is never stopped; a 30-member Terwillegar 4 h ensemble took 515 s on the workstation), so on the 1 shared vCPU the new multi-day run had no frames for minutes and the old one stayed in view. **Fix:** (a) one `leaveCurrentRun` path for every new run (single, multi-day, perimeter restart) and Clear results: the ensemble poll stops and the app sends `DELETE /simulations/{id}/ensemble`, which stops the ensemble before its next member (`run_ensemble(should_stop=…)`, status `cancelled`); (b) a single run with the hourly forecast clears the previous run as soon as Run is pressed (`prepareRun`), not after the forecast fetch; (c) WebSocket events for another simulation ID are ignored (late POST responses already were, by run token); (d) `useEnsemble` treats `cancelled` as off. Not done: the server does not stop an abandoned ensemble on its own (a client that never sends DELETE, e.g. a closed tab, still leaves it running) | Owner report 2026-10-11; `frontend/src/hooks/rerun.test.ts`, `frontend/tests/e2e/rerun.spec.ts`, API `test_cancel_*`, engine `test_should_stop_cancels_before_the_next_member` |
| 2026-10-11 | **Bug: multi-day runs very slow (live: 2 days, uniform C-2, 120 min snapshots, burning 10-20 sat at hour 2 of 48 for minutes).** Profiled locally (engine only, `_execute_multiday`): the same request takes 11.7 s on the workstation, so the engine alone does not explain minutes; contention does (above: the previous run's ensemble in the same 1-vCPU process; locally a concurrent ensemble slowed the run only 1.2x, but on the shared Fly CPU it competes for the whole burst budget — not measured on Fly). Engine cost: 70 % in `simplify_front`: an O(n²) angular resample (a `min()` scan with `atan2` for every target angle over every hull vertex, ~14.5 M calls for 2 days), a redundant angle sort of every wavelet point (~7,600 points a step), and per-vertex FBP and trigonometry for wavelets that are identical in uniform fuel. **Fix (results unchanged):** bisection over precomputed angles with `min()`'s tie-breaking, the hull loop on plain floats, one wavelet per distinct (fuel, slope, aspect, modifier, canopy) per step. Frames are bit-identical on 8 configs (multi-day C-2 with/without DEM, D-1 3 days no burning period, O-1b, Edmonton grid 3 days; single C-2 4 h, M-1 8 h, O-1b 6 h); times: 2-day C-2 11.7 → 5.7 s, with DEM 9.7 → 5.6 s, 3-day D-1 33.8 → 17.9 s, O-1b 13.8 → 7.5 s, single C-2 4 h 3.5 → 1.4 s. No burning-period wait loop or snapshot problem in the Huygens path (no-spread periods step at 5 min with zero distance, cheap); the grid path already jumps to the next weather change. **Memory:** the DEM's slope/aspect were lists of Python floats, ~560 MB for the Edmonton DEM (4.8 M cells) held in the grid cache for the process lifetime; now `array('d')` rows (~76 MB, same values). **Edmonton grid, 3 days, burning 10-20:** 21 s locally (CFL-limited level-set steps, unchanged), peak RSS 713 → 425 MB; without burning period 76 s, 361 MB. Frames streamed: multi-day accepts `cells_mode: "incremental"` (whole frame at each day start), 21.5 → 1.65 MB for the 3-day run | Owner report 2026-10-11; [R20]; engine `test_hull_and_resampling_match_the_reference`, `test_front_expansion_shares_wavelets_without_changing_them`, `test_float_rows_are_compact_and_exact`; API `test_multiday_incremental_frames_rebuild_cumulative` |

| 2026-10-11 | **Buildings for all of Alberta, read by window** (owner-approved task). (1) Data: Microsoft Canadian Building Footprints, Alberta (2019 release; the Edmonton extract is exactly its footprints with ring-average centroid in 53.39-53.71 N, 113.75-113.25 W, same coordinates), one FlatGeobuf with a packed Hilbert R-tree, fields `id` (Edmonton ids kept, others 1,000,000 + source position) and `area_m2`; no address fields. FlatGeobuf over GeoParquet: box reads through the R-tree touch only the matching features with no in-memory state, pyogrio (bundled GDAL) reads it locally or via `/vsicurl/`; GeoParquet would need pyarrow and decodes whole row groups. (2) Statistics Canada ODB v3 evaluated and **not used**: in five municipal windows 94-99 % of Microsoft footprints are in ODB, ODB adds 5-37 % (mostly < 20 m² accessory buildings and newer houses), but it covers only 11 municipalities (+ some federal footprints), carries per-municipality licences and address fields, and mixing it would change the building unit in some towns and break Edmonton equivalence; a per-municipality switch is an owner decision. (3) Engine/API: `BuildingIndex` keeps the Edmonton 4-nearest-neighbourhood selection exactly (former extract box; windows = neighbourhood polygons + Voronoi cells), structure spread reads the reachable box (clipped to the extract box for Edmonton runs), runs elsewhere use the buildings in the run grid box (≤ `FIRESIM_BUILDINGS_MAX_NEAR` 30,000 nearest); the request path `edmonton_buildings.geojson.gz` is resolved to the Alberta file when installed. (4) Community units outside Edmonton: StatCan 2021 census subdivisions (named places), not dissemination areas (unnamed codes). (5) Vegetation around buildings outside Edmonton stays on demand (`build_building_vegetation.py ring-metrics` per area), not province-wide | Owner task 2026-10-11. Reproduced with Playwright on the mocked API: with an incident open, run → the completed run is saved into Day 1 (`finalPerimeter` = last frame `perimeter`, **[lat, lng]**) → EOC Console → + New Period → Advance; Day 2 draws Day 1's perimeter as a ghost and `EOCConsole` read the pairs as [lng, lat], so `map.project` got latitude −113.6 and MapLibre threw `Error: Invalid LngLat latitude value: must be between -90 and 90` (stack: `LngLat` ← `LngLat.convert` ← `Map.project` ← `EOCConsole` ghost-perimeter `map` ← React render). No error boundary above the EOC tab, so React unmounted the whole app (`#root` empty). Same crash on opening the EOC tab for any stored incident whose active period is Day 2+ after a completed Day 1 run. Present since the multi-day incident store (TRA-216, `f9b6265`); not caused by PRs #48/#55-#58 (the EOC path reads no `fuel_source`/`buildings_source`/community fields). **Fix:** (a) `utils/ghostPerimeter.ts` projects the stored ring as [lat, lng], skips non-finite/out-of-range points and drops the ghost (console warning) if projection throws; (b) `OperationalPeriodPanel` reads stored incidents defensively (missing `date`/`frameSummaries`/`areaHa`, invalid date for the next-period default, now `nextPeriodDate` in `utils/time.ts` on the Edmonton calendar); `advancePeriod` tolerates a stale `activePeriodIndex` and makes the appended period active with the next day number; (c) `AppErrorBoundary`: top level (`main.tsx`, "Return to map" reloads; incidents restore from localStorage) and around the EOC tab (`App.tsx`, "Return to map" switches to Simulation; resets on tab change); the error and component stack are logged to the console | Owner report 2026-10-11; `frontend/src/utils/ghostPerimeter.test.ts`, `components/OperationalPeriodPanel.test.tsx`, `components/AppErrorBoundary.test.tsx`, `tests/e2e/eoc-period.spec.ts` |

| 2026-10-11 | **Run archive, phase 1 (storage option A).** Design as proposed in [R28] §6 with these choices: (1) the archive stores the **serialised live GET body** (plus `/arrival`, `/ensemble` bodies) rather than engine frames, so an archived GET is shape-identical by construction (incl. multi-day `day`/`day_fwi` and incremental `cells_offset`) and needs no second serialiser; (2) archiving is hooked on the **end of the run thread**, not on the status change, because a run is "completed" before its ensemble finishes; one worker thread, failures logged only; failed runs are archived too (audit), paused-forever runs are not; (3) payloads are read from the object store even without an index row, and `meta.json` copies the row, so a lost index loses no run; (4) the local backend is the default (tests, development, Fly without a volume: temp dir + warning); S3 via boto3 as an optional extra, falling back to local without credentials; (5) Litestream 0.5.17 in the image, used only when `FIRESIM_ARCHIVE_DB` and the R2 secrets exist, with a config check and a bounded (120 s) restore so it can never stop the API; (6) per-building fields kept exactly as the live GET returns them (centroid exposure, illustrative structure polygons; no addresses), not stripped, so a reloaded run displays the same; no export or 209 reads the archive; (7) no deploy, volume, bucket or secret created (owner approval). | Owner approval of option A phase 1, 2026-10-11; [R28] §6; [R30]; tests §5 |

## 4. Methods and references

### 4.1 Method → source

| Component | Implementation | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Long-range ember spot fires in the grid model (opt-in, v6; not in the API) | `spread/ember_spotting.py` (`LongRangeSpotter`), `cellular.run_cellular_simulation(spot_hook=…)`, `_seed_spot` | Generation Qin 2025 eq 3.7 pp.59-61 [V], f_s Filkov et al. 2017 Table 1 p.3267 [D]; transport v5 two-mode kernel: Sardoy et al. 2008 Fig. 10 p.488 [V] + long mode located by Storey et al. 2020 *Fire* 3:10 p.7 [V]; establishment η · p_e (Beverly & Wotton 2007 eq 2.02 p.5, preprint) with η **fitted** [H form]; spot distance measured as Storey p.6. Spec §6.8, [R47] |
| v6 community-arrival validation (target A) | `scripts/long_range_v6_arrival.py`, `long_range_v6_data.py` | Observed arrival intervals from NIST TN 2135 Appendix F (Maranghides et al. 2021), GOFER perimeters, FIRIS perimeters, VIIRS 375 m (previous overpass as lower bound [H], spec C42); 540 m community blocks [H]; town ROS × 0.5 [H] (C40; observed within-community rates Masoudvaziri et al. 2021 Table 1 p.2) |
| v6 building-loss combined model (target B) | `scripts/long_range_v6_loss.py` | Logistic regression; density within 100 m (Knapp et al. 2021 preprint p.10), DINS year built (C43), canopy 0-30 m (v4 rings; 0-9 m not available), A-model reach and landed embers; realism reference Zamanialaei et al. 2025 Tables 1-2 p.7 (C41, partly verified) |
| v7 calibrated front (stage 1) | `scripts/long_range_v7_arrival.py` (`front-grid`, `fit-front`), `long_range_v7_fuels.py` | LANDFIRE FBFM40 (Rollins 2009; Scott & Burgan 2005 groups) → FBP crosswalk [H]; hourly RAWS wind (10 m = f_w × 20 ft, Andrews 2009 p.58); hourly FFMC Van Wagner 1977; m_G, m_S, m_T, f_w fitted [H form] |
| v7 variable ember establishment (stage 2) | `spread/ember_spotting.py` (η0 · w(group) · p_e(category, FFMC)), `long_range_v7_arrival.py fit-eta` | Beverly & Wotton 2007 eqs 2.02-2.03 p.5 (grass / pine p_e); η0, κ fitted [H form] |
| v7 building-loss model (target B) | `scripts/long_range_v7_loss.py` | v6 model + log NN separation, canopy 0-9 m (NAIP NDVI, v7 data agents), wildland-edge flag, fire-level wind (log p90 hourly 10 m wind), landed embers × edge / interior |
| FBP fuel types, ROS, BUI effect, SFC, CFB, TFC, HFI, LB, back/flank ROS, net effective wind + slope, FMC from date, point-ignition acceleration | `fbp/calculator.py`, `fbp/constants.py`, `fbp/crown_fire.py` | ST-X-3 [1] eqs 1-8, 26-58, 70-73, 81; GLC-X-10 [2] (grass curing eqs 35a/b, M-4 `c`, revised forms); verified against `cffdrs` [6] |
| D-2 no spread below BUI 80 | `fbp/calculator.py` | Alexander (2010) [7] **as cited by cffdrs**; full reference from the reference lists of de Groot et al. (2022) and CFS NOR-X-433 (2026-10-10); the paper itself not read |
| Crown fire initiation, CFB | `fbp/crown_fire.py` | Van Wagner (1977) crown fire [3]; ST-X-3 eqs 56-58. Context only (not implemented): the physically based CFIM of Cruz et al. (2006) [50, 51] finds flame-zone depth and the fuel strata gap dominate and FMC matters much less than Van Wagner assumes (Part II pp.61, 70) |
| FWI System (FFMC, DMC, DC, ISI, BUI, FWI) | `fwi/calculator.py` | Van Wagner & Pickett (1985) [5]; Van Wagner (1987) [5b]: FFMC scale eqs 2a/2b (printed p. 4, PDF p. 15); DMC scale eq 16 (printed p. 10, PDF p. 21) and rain eqs 17-19 (printed p. 11); DC eqs 22-26 (printed pp. 13-14, PDF pp. 24-25) with Lf Table 3 (printed p. 15). Cold-day DC floor T ≥ −2.8 °C as in cffdrs [6] (from 2026-10-10). Verified against cffdrs on the 48-day test sequence + 21 edge cases (`engine/tests/fwi/test_cffdrs_fwi_reference.py`) |

**Deliberate deviations and heuristics**
- FFMC moisture coefficient 147.2 (Van Wagner 1987 eq 2; ST-X-3 eq 46) in the engine (FWI and FBP) and, from 2026-10-10, in the frontend's live ISI (was 147.27723); cffdrs uses 147.27723. **Effect (corrected 2026-10-10; was stated as "ISI changes ≤ 1e-3"):** for a given FFMC, ISI moves by ≤ 0.1 % (relative), so the frontend change is cosmetic; through the daily FFMC recursion the codes differ from cffdrs-147.27723 by up to 0.12 FFMC, 0.26 ISI, 0.25 FWI on the 48-day sequence, and 0.67 ISI / 0.52 FWI on single days near FFMC 97; in hourly FFMC chains up to 1.12 FFMC (480 h cffdrs `hffmc` test, [R15]). With the coefficient swapped the FFMC/ISI/DC differences vanish (tested). The cffdrs fixtures are generated with 147.2 (the FWI fixture also at 147.27723).
- DMC after rain uses Van Wagner (1987) eq 16 as printed (M = 20 + exp(5.6348 − P/43.43)); cffdrs uses the program form 20 + 280/exp(0.023 P). Up to 0.064 DMC difference on rain days (48-day sequence); kept, open item §6.
- Spotting: emission, probability, landing below the maximum, and per-fuel stand sizes (`STAND_DEFAULTS`) are assumptions; terrain correction not implemented; active crown fires use the torching model with several trees (underestimate). The active crown fire model of Albini, Alexander & Cruz (2012) is **not** implemented.
- Ensemble perturbations are symmetric (median 1): the area bias is not corrected, so burn probability is over-confident; curing and FMC sizes are not validated. Per M3 (2026-10-10) the area bias is to be fixed through set-up, and any ROS factor only by fire type and fuel.
- Burning period is a fixed clock window (no overnight runs, ROS × 0 outside it).
- DMC and DC are held fixed within a run.
- Building exposure: blackbody 1200 K, emissivity 1 even for thin surface flames; no embers, no convective heating, no burning buildings or yard fuels.
- Validation harness: CFSDS DOB days are interpolated from satellite passes (hours of timing uncertainty); one ERA5 weather point per fire; 2014b national fuel grid without burn-scar succession; D-1/M-1 → D-2/M-2 between day-of-year 150 and 258 (assumption for Alberta boreal); M-1/M-2 at 50 % conifer; O-1 curing 90 % outside the green season / 60 % inside (the M1 check [R14] also ran 95 % in the spring window and 60 % everywhere).
- WUI zone modifiers in `data/wui_zones.geojson.gz` are unsourced and off by default.

- [R29] *Alberta assets roads terrain* (2026-10-11), `~/dev/wildfire/reports/Alberta assets roads terrain 2026-10-11.md` (data and figures `~/dev/wildfire/reports/data/alberta-assets-roads-terrain-2026-10-11/`, `make_figures.py`): sources and licences, build and file sizes / SHA-256, ODHF merge, coverage by town, terrain build and selection, memory before/after on the local API, Hinton and Canmore before/after runs, owner release steps.
- CAL FIRE DINS test data (2026-10-10), `~/dev/wildfire/reports/CAL FIRE DINS independent test data 2026-10-10.md` (data agent's report; Eaton and Camp units, vegetation, seeds, wind; descriptive roof table).

### 4.4 Validation results (summary of `docs/validation.md`, 2026-10-07 to 2026-10-09)

- **Long-range embers v7, PREREG_LR7 (2026-10-17)** [R48]: fresh held-out Dixie (decides) + Caldor, 72 blocks. A1 calibrated front vs v6 front −0.44 h (−1.60, +0.47) → not met; A2 embers MAE +0.31 h (+0.07, +0.67), Brier +0.187 (+0.153, +0.225) → **FAIL**; Dixie biases −6.6/−7.5/−8.0 h (A0v6/A0c/V7). A3 tail 0.19, max 42 km. Target B fire-averaged AUC B_dens 0.576 (= B_dens_age; no year built), B_comb_A0c 0.651, B_comb_V7 0.643 → B1 +0.067 (+0.017, +0.125) **met**, B2 −0.008 not met, < 0.70. Secondary (never decide): A1 −4.08 h (−5.39, −2.71), A2 MAE −0.65 h (−1.79, +0.45), Brier +0.087 (+0.057, +0.116); B1 +0.065, B2 −0.016.
- **Long-range embers v6, PREREG_LR6 (2026-10-15)** [R47]: held-out Glass, CZU, North Complex. Target A (community arrival, 279 blocks): MAE V6 11.17 h vs A0 10.64 h, Δ +0.53 h (95 % +0.34, +0.75); Brier Δ +0.021 (+0.015, +0.027) → **FAIL**; A0 itself ~10 h off (proxy-fuel front too fast on Glass/North Complex, too slow on CZU). Spot tail ratio 0.106 (Storey 0.20; band met), max 10.7 km. Target B: fire-averaged AUC density 0.449, density+age 0.507, combined 0.603 (A0 reach) / 0.604 (V6) → B1 +0.096 (+0.068, +0.125) **met**; embers +0.0001; below the 0.70 reference.
- **20 m WUI window (2026-10-10; M5; model behaviour, not a validation)** [R18]: CFSDS held-out validation not rerun (its 250 m national grid is refined to 90 m, so nearest neighbour stays and inputs are byte-identical; no buildings, so the window never applies); Jasper not rerun (structure-only, no fuel grid). Uniform fuel, 1 h: area vs FBP ellipse 50 m / 20 m: C-2 −4.4 / −4.3 % (W wind), −6.3 / −3.8 % (SW); O-1a −9.5 / −6.5 % (W), −10.1 / −7.8 % (SSW), −7.8 / −5.8 % (SW). Six Edmonton runs, 6 h, area 50 m nearest / 50 m mode / 20 m: A 2.0 / 0.0 / 10.2 and 11.0 / 7.0 / 22.3 ha; B 21.0 / 15.5 / 21.1 and 59.3 / 61.8 / 82.1; C 32.3 / 30.3 / 59.1 and 94.8 / 115.5 / 149.5 (moderate / extreme). Without the building mask the 20 m areas change ≤ 3 %, so the increase is fuel detail. Front-contact gap median 22-42 → 9-15 m, max 64-78 → 24-30 m; involved at 6 h −52 % to +33 % vs [R16]; contact 20 m now +9 to +68 % (meaningful again at 20 m); 0 ember ignitions in all 42 runs.

- **Data:** 143 fire-days, 32 Alberta fires 2014-2024 (CFSDS), one burn day from the observed perimeter, Bennett et al. (2026) protocol, grid model, deterministic, no suppression.
- **Baseline (first round):** one-day F1 at the default 06-23 h window **0.15** (whole perimeter start, operational) and **0.24** with Bennett's ignition (W.I.S.E. defaults 0.26 nationally; tuned W.I.S.E. 0.54). Growth over-predicted on 93 % of days (perimeter start), normalised area difference +0.67; head bearing error median ~50°; forward spread within ±35 % on ~20 % of days. On nine shared fire-days with identical inputs: W.I.S.E. 0.19 vs FireSim 0.24.
- **Held-out (second round):** fires split by seeded hash (16 calibration / 16 test fires, 79 test fire-days), settings chosen on calibration only. Active edges (previous 2 days) + FFMC spin-up + burning period 10-20 h: test F1 **0.118 → 0.208** (ΔF1 +0.091, 95 % CI +0.056 to +0.125, bootstrap by fire); area difference +0.72 → +0.27; spread within ±35 % on 24 % of days. Oracle start 0.245. Largest wind-driven runs (Horse River 4-5 May 2016) under-predicted further.
- **Grid diagonal-spread fix (2026-10-09, PR #35):** re-run of the chosen set-up and the Bennett start, all 143 fire-days, before (`a8f8f35`) and after (`4bcfb1d`); "before" reproduced the numbers above exactly. Held-out 17 h F1: active edges + spin-up + 10-20 h **0.208 → 0.212** (ΔF1 +0.004, 95 % CI −0.001 to +0.009); Bennett start + spin-up 0.245 → 0.250 (+0.005, +0.000 to +0.011). Earlier windows dip slightly (8 h −0.003 / −0.008; best hour −0.004 / −0.007, CIs exclude zero but tiny). Skill effectively unchanged; the fix is a correctness fix (grid-orientation independence), not a skill change [R5].
- **Ensemble (third round, held-out; measured before the grid fix):** 20 members on the chosen set-up, 65 test fire-days (14 largest excluded by run time only). Calibrated sizes vs former placeholders: observed area inside members' P10-P90 **20 % → 49 %** (ideal ~80 %); CRPS of log10 area 0.546 → **0.471** (single run 0.616); Brier skill vs single run +0.18 → +0.25; spread/skill 4.7 → 1.6 (still too narrow); cells at ~95 % burn probability burned 33 % of the time; P50 F1 0.201 vs single run 0.195; **P10 footprint held ≥ 90 % of observed growth on 29 % of days**, so P10 is not a worst case.
- **Structure-spread sensitivity (2026-10-09; model sensitivity, not a validation)** [R7]: Hamada option, Edmonton footprints (334,192 units), 3 WUI sites (mature ravine edge with garages; lower-density ravine edge; newer suburb beside grass) × 2 days (FWI 15 and 72), 6 h, deterministic FBP run shared by all variants. Involved buildings at 6 h vs the defaults (30 m cutoff, 10 m contact, all footprints), over the 5 runs with a non-zero default: cutoff 20 m **−48 % to 0 %**, 45 m **+5 % to +220 %** (between 30 and 45 m the unit graph joins blocks across streets: largest component 2.5 % → 7.5 % of units); contact 5 m **−71 % to 0 %**, 20 m **+1 % to +557 %** (and 0 → 148 on one moderate run); dropping footprints < 40 m² **−8 % to 0 %**. The contact result is grid-dependent: on the 50 m engine grid with the all-touched building mask the nearest burnable cell lies 0-50 m from a footprint depending on where it sits in its cell. Defaults unchanged; a change to how contact is measured is recommended (§6). City-wide graph at 30 m: median nearest separation 2.56 m (2.58 m without < 40 m²), median degree 8, 1.6 % with no neighbour. Run time: whole city units 3-6 s, spread < 0.3 s per variant; the script's default reproduces the engine's own counts exactly and repeats identically.
- **Structure front contact from the building cells (2026-10-09; model sensitivity, not a validation)** [R8]: same sites, days, seed and FBP runs as [R7] (a rerun of the unchanged script reproduced [R7] exactly). With contact measured from each building's grid cells (`11c1230`): contact 5 / 20 m **0 %** in all six runs (the contact distance is below the 50 m grid's resolution); involved buildings at 6 h vs the first rule **+38 % to +751 %** (site A moderate 0 → 199; front contacts 2-6×; front-contacted footprints a median 22-42 m, at most ~70 m, from the nearest burned cell edge); cutoff 20 m **−58 % to −27 %**, 45 m **+4 % to +170 %**; dropping footprints < 40 m² **−12 % to 0 %**. The cutoff is now the main structural sensitivity; defaults unchanged. The script's default again matches the engine in all runs; the contact rule costs ~2 s per variant over the whole city.
- **Jasper 2024 structure check (2026-10-10; first test against observed Canadian losses)** [R10]: end-state, structure-only, ember entry imposed, pre-registered (§3).
  - **Primary run:** 15 seeds at 18:00, cutoff 30 m, wind 15 km/h SW, 18:00-24:00. 396 involved against 370 destroyed (358 official). TP 250 / FP 146 / FN 120 / TN 603; precision **63.1 %**, recall **67.6 %**, F1 65.3 %, **κ 0.472**.
  - **Baselines (κ):**

    | Baseline | κ |
    |---|---|
    | Count-matched distance band from the seeds | **0.516** |
    | Distance band with N = observed | 0.499 |
    | FPI < 5 m, one step | 0.025 |
    | FPI ≤ 5 m percolation | 0.095 |
    | Random at the observed rate | ≈ 0 |
    | Seeds only (no spread) | 0.011 |

  - **κ differences (250 m block bootstrap):** FireSim − band −0.044 (95 % −0.157 to +0.045); FireSim − seeds only +0.461 (+0.238 to +0.649).
  - **Pre-registered rule not met:** given the ember-entry locations, Hamada does no better than distance from them.
  - **Sensitivity (12 pre-specified runs):**
    - κ 0.19-0.51, precision 55-84 %, recall 22-92 %, involved count 124-593;
    - the cutoff dominates: 20 m gives 124 (κ 0.19), 45 m gives 593 (κ 0.51);
    - wind ±25 % and wind from SSW change little, because below 10 m/s Hamada is nearly isotropic (Hazus blend);
    - FireSim − band runs from −0.044 to +0.068, and every interval includes zero.
  - **Seeds and outcome:** scoring without the seeds, or with Destroyed + Visible Damage, changes κ by ≤ 0.03.
  - **Errors** sit at 250-500 m from the seeds:
    - FN: separate ember-ignited groups (not modelled);
    - FP: at the north-east front towards the commercial core, where the after-action review records demolitions at 21:30 (not recorded, so not testable).
  - **Graph:** at 5 / 20 / 30 m the largest component holds 1.6 / 8.6 / 49.7 % of units.
  - **Context only:** precision is in the range of FSJ104651 (59 / 9 / 77 %).
- **Grass curing default (2026-10-10; M1)** [R14]: chosen set-up, all 143 fire-days, only O-1 curing changed.
  - **Held-out 17 h F1:** 60 % everywhere (old default) **0.2126** → M1 (95 % in day of year 60-149; 60 % summer and 90 % fall as stand-ins for the user's entry) **0.2120**; ΔF1 −0.0007 (95 % CI −0.0017 to −0.0000). Area difference +0.246 → +0.248. Calibration 0.2251 → 0.2285. The former harness rule (90 / 60) gives 0.2120 and reproduces the recorded number.
  - **Spring-window fire-days** (the only ones M1 changes, with 2 fall days): 5 held-out from 3 fires −0.009 [−0.016, +0.000]; 9 calibration (6 Horse River) +0.024 [−0.010, +0.135], mostly one day where 60 % stopped the fire.
  - **Grass involvement:** O-1 is 3.4 % of all observed growth; present in the growth of 76 of 143 fire-days, ≥ 5 % of the growth on 23. Boreal fires: the harness cannot test the 95 % value.
  - **Sensitivity:** a silent 90 % in summer lowers held-out F1 (−0.008 [−0.019, −0.000]) and raises area over-prediction (+0.25 → +0.30), which supports no summer default.
- **Jasper 2024 ember check (2026-10-10; pre-registered, `ccce4d6`)** [R16]: Hamada + embers on the same set-up as [R10] (structure-only; no wildland ember source).
  - **No building was ignited by embers in any of the 17 runs** (150 / 400 kW/m², GR' 5.68 / 10, cutoff 20 / 30 / 45 m, wind × 0.75 / × 1.25 / 27 km/h gust, embers without Hamada). Each Hamada + embers run equals its Hamada-only reference: primary κ **0.472** (precision 63.1 %, recall 67.6 %), distance band 0.516, difference −0.044 (−0.157 to +0.045). Decision rule not met.
  - Model-only diagnostic: at 15 km/h ψ* = 0.178 g/cm² (~1.4 million embers on a median 154 m² footprint) against 0.46 million emitted by such a building over its whole 150 kW/m² fire (2.4 million at 400); the largest ember pool on any building not otherwise involved reached 4.5 % (150) / 14 % (400) of its threshold. Embers alone ignite a few buildings only at 400 kW/m² and ≥ 40 km/h.
- **Edmonton ember rerun (2026-10-10; model sensitivity, not a validation)** [R16]: `scripts/structure_sensitivity.py --embers`, same 3 sites x 2 days, seed and FBP runs as [R7]/[R8] (Hamada counts reproduced: 199 / 315 / 83 / 454 / 308 / 653 at 6 h). Seven ember variants (default 150 kW/m² with wildland embers; GR' 5.68; 400 kW/m²; cutoff 20 / 45 m; structures-only at 150 and 400): **0 ember-ignited buildings in all 42 runs**, involved counts identical to the matching Hamada variants, including the extreme days (max HFI 78-82 MW/m, wildland Sardoy embers on). The engine's reachable build matched the whole-city build in all six default ember runs (2,606-5,576 units built instead of 334,192).

The pre-registered decision metrics (Brier, κ at P ≥ 0.5) do not depend on order. The same tie-break exists in the step-3 count-matched baselines (`structure_step3_validation.py`, Camp (a)); future pre-registrations should break ties with a seeded permutation.

Equal F1 despite this shows one-day F1 depends weakly on fuel type here.

- **Not yet measured:** RPAS-corrected mid-day restarts, real thermal-flight active edges, ensemble skill on the largest runs; the curing default against Edmonton grass-fire records; field accuracy of any fuel grid.

## 5. Testing

| Suite | Command | What it checks |
|---|---|---|
| Engine (pytest) | `PYTHONPATH=engine/src:api/src python -m pytest engine/tests -q` | FBP vs cffdrs fixture, FWI + hourly FFMC, FBP ellipse agreement, WISE reference, level set, deployment data (head/flank/back), active edges, diurnal, ensemble, Albini worked examples, Cohen exposure, loaders/reprojection, national code schemes, validation metrics |
| API (pytest) | `PYTHONPATH=engine/src:api/src python -m pytest api/tests -q` | Endpoints, WebSocket frames, grid runs, FWI, weather, skill options |
| Frontend types | `cd frontend && npx tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.app.json` | TypeScript (the root `tsconfig.json` has `files: []` + references, so plain `npx tsc --noEmit` checks nothing; CI's `npm run build` runs `tsc -b`) |
| Frontend unit (Vitest) | `cd frontend && npm test` | Classes, contrast, time zone, evac arrival, assets, ICS outputs, skill options, curing default |
| Frontend E2E (Playwright + axe) | `cd frontend && npx playwright test` | Production build via `vite preview`; API mocked by `tests/e2e/mockApi.ts` replaying `tests/fixtures/terwillegar_grass_4h.json` (house-to-house spread: `structure_spread_4h.json`, `record_fixture.py --structure`); external requests blocked; axe baseline, 12 px / 24 px layout rules, idle-redraw performance budget |
| Pyra parity | `PYTHONPATH=engine/src:api/src python -m pytest api/tests/test_pyra_source.py`; with `PYRA_DIR=<Pyra checkout>` and node, Pyra's engine is re-run live | FireSim's "pyra" tier vs Pyra's own engine (`initFWI` in Node via Pyra's test harness, `scripts/pyra/run_pyra_parity.mjs`) on recorded inputs (`api/tests/fixtures/pyra_parity.json`, built by `scripts/pyra/build_parity_fixture.py`); daily-step equations; caching, fallbacks, labels |
| Validation (not CI) | `scripts/validate.py prepare / run / report / compare` | Skill on observed fires (§4.4) |

**Oracle / reference provenance**
- **cffdrs daily FWI fixture** `engine/tests/fwi/data/cffdrs_fwi_reference.json`, generated by `generate_cffdrs_fwi_reference.py` from `cffdrs_py` (`CFFDRS_PY_PATH`; `cffdrs.fwi`, `lat_adjust=False`): the cffdrs `tests/data/fwi_test_data.csv` 48-day sequence (the R package's `test_fwi`, lat 40, April-June 1985, start 85/6/15) with its one-decimal values, and 21 edge cases, each at 147.2 and 147.27723. Tolerances: FFMC/ISI/DC 1e-9; DMC 0.07, BUI 0.07, FWI 0.025 (DMC rain-phase form, §4.1), exact without DMC rain.
- **cffdrs fixture** `engine/tests/fbp/data/` generated by `engine/tests/fbp/data/generate_cffdrs_reference.py` from a `cffdrs_py` checkout (`CFFDRS_PY_PATH`), FFMC coefficient 147.2; 18 fuels × 6 weather/slope cases in CI, 12,960 cases in a development run; hourly FFMC 5,760 cases (max diff 3e-11).
- **WISE reference** `engine/tests/spread/data/wise_reference.json`: WISE 1.0.6-beta.6 (official Ubuntu build, headless) on uniform C-2 / O-1a, wind 0/20/30 km/h, flat and sloped planes (built at 30.5/40.5 % because WISE truncates slope to integer percent).
- **Cohen worked values:** SIAM 50 m × 20 m flame at 1200 K → 79 / 45 / 27 kW/m² at 10 / 20 / 30 m and ~60 s at 31 kW/m² (Cohen 2004); FireSim 80.6 / 45.9 / 27.8 and 59 s.
- **Albini/Chase worked examples:** Chase 1981 torching 0.34 mi; Albini 1983 surface fire 0.45 km.
- **CFSDS validation:** Barber et al. (2024) DOB rasters; metrics unit-tested on shapes with known answers; W.I.S.E. comparison numbers from Bennett et al. (2026) Table 1 plus nine local W.I.S.E. runs.
- **Frontend fixture:** recorded from the real engine by `npm run fixture:record` (`frontend/tests/fixtures/record_fixture.py`).

**Previous results (PR #56), CI race fix (2026-10-11, branch `fix/rerun-and-multiday`):** CI (Python 3.12) failed `test_multiday_incremental_frames_rebuild_cumulative` with 22 of 25 frames under status "completed". Cause, a real API race (not the test): `GET /simulations/{id}` read the frames and then the status, so a run finishing while the frames were serialised was reported "completed" with its last frames missing (a polling client then stops with an incomplete run); the WebSocket replay had the same order. Fix: the status is read before the frames in GET and in the WebSocket replay (frames are always added before the terminal status), and a run that fails after the replay snapshot now gets its `simulation.error`. Regression test `test_get_never_reports_completed_with_frames_missing` (the run finishes exactly between the two reads). API pytest then **201 passed, 1 skipped**.

## 6. Open items

- [ ] **Long-range embers v7 follow-ups** [R48] (owner rule: push, do not park): (1) the calibrated front transfers poorly (late on training fires, 7.5 h early on Dixie; 17 Dixie blocks observed 25-34 h are reached within 7 h by every model): look at suppression / overnight slowdown / run-window timing before refitting rates; (2) ember establishment counts are unconstrained (the same η gives 30-130 vs 5,600 spot fires per member): calibrate against observed spot-fire counts (e.g. NIST TN 2135 Fig. 50/51) rather than arrival alone; (3) the v5 Sardoy short-range kernel extrapolates to medians of 10^5-10^8 m at extreme intensity — cap the intensity or the distance per the source's fitted range; (4) target B is uncalibrated on fresh fires (Brier 0.63 vs base 0.36) and year built is missing on Dixie/Caldor.
- [ ] **Long-range embers v6 follow-ups** [R47] (owner rule: push, do not park): (1) the grid front decides community-arrival timing and is ~10 h off with single proxy fuels — real fuels (LANDFIRE FBFM40 / FBP crosswalk, pre-fire), hourly observed wind direction, a spread-rate factor calibrated on training arrival, then re-fit η; (2) η depending on receptivity and FFMC rather than one value; (3) a fire-level loss rate for target B and a separate wildland-edge ember term (landed embers rank loss on CZU 0.66 and North Complex 0.64 held-out, but backwards on training); (4) a 0-9 m canopy ring; (5) digitise NIST TN 2135 Fig. 50/51 (65 Paradise spot fires) as a California spot-distance check; (6) expose spot_hook spotting in the API only after (1).
- [ ] **Re-check opt-in spotting results made before `64e6ac7`** (spot fires outside the burned box never spread): any reported spotting runs under-state spotting.

Building mask on the fuel grid still covers only the 4 nearest neighbourhoods, while structure units may come from the whole run area (the building-cell contact rule needs no special case for it: outside the mask a building's own cells can burn; a mask over every building would change the wildland run itself, so it is not done here). 12,025 of the 346,238 footprints have their centroid outside the Edmonton fuel grid's box and never become units.
- [ ] **Ember ignition (2026-10-10, [R16]): owner decision needed on the design fire for the ember stage.** At the 150 kW/m² default (D1) a building's whole fire emits about as many embers per m² of footprint as the threshold needs at 40 mph, so ember ignition almost never happens; GR' = 10 [T] was set by its authors with the 400 kW/m² curve (Qin 2025 p.147, Table 6.1). Options: keep 150 (ember stage effectively inert, honest to D1), use 400 for the ember stage only, or wait for a Canadian test. Also open: the wind heights in the Himoto and Sardoy PDFs are inferred from check values (C17, C18); Sardoy applied to 50 m FBP cells at crown-fire intensities is outside its shown range; the 250-500 m Jasper losses are not explained by this short-range model.
- [ ] **Burn-out duration (2026-10-10, [R19]):** 66 / 70 min is the published design fire's length, not a measured burning time for Edmonton construction (Qin25 p.146: ~50 min for a model house; FSJ104686 p.2: tens of minutes to hours). It binds only for crossings longer than that, so results barely depend on it; if a later stage (WU-E heat flux, embers at 400 kW/m²) is driven by the HRR curve, its shape matters more than its end. Owner decision open on whether burn-out stays on by default (on now, for one design fire across stages).

Forecast T/RH, the real source of FFMC error during a run, are not perturbed; the 1.5 inflation was fitted with this set-up, so it absorbs the gap, but members do not experience "FFMC sd 7.2". The measured GEM day-1 biases (FFMC −3.1, log wind speed −0.15; `docs/validation.md`) are not applied. Fix: perturb the hourly T/RH stream with sizes from `input_errors.py` (same GEM-vs-station data) or perturb FFMC at the start of the burning period; apply or document the biases; refit the inflation.

Not implemented in PR #50.
- [ ] **Pyra tier, known gaps (2026-10-10):** (a) after noon LST with CWFIS empty, Pyra uses an MSC SWOB noon observation before GEM; FireSim goes straight to GEM (message says so); (b) Alberta only: Pyra's BC page (BCWS noon mirror tier, PST) is not reproduced, BC points use the CWFIS chain; (c) Pyra's IDW mode (CWFIS + AEF blend) and a browser's older holding cache are not reproduced (default page only); (d) the station list and rules are a copy of Pyra `38aee4f`: re-export the list and re-run the parity test with `PYRA_DIR` after any Pyra change to the chain; (e) the frontend computes its live ISI/FWI from the wind rounded to 1 km/h, so the panel can differ from the API (and Pyra) in the last decimal of ISI/FWI on windy dry days — the starting FFMC/DMC/DC are exact; (f) Alberta moves to permanent UTC-6 on 2026-11-01: Pyra's `lstOffset 7` / `noonUTC 19` and FireSim's copy must change together, and how CWFIS reports noon after that is unverified; (g) the peak-burn ISI/FWI (16:00 MDT wind) are returned but not used by the run.
- [ ] **Current weather after the 2026-10-10 fixes:** outside Alberta (or when Pyra's chain has no value) the off-season value is still a one-day cold start from 85/6/15 (DMC, DC, BUI far too low except after snowmelt; labelled); a carry-over chain (stepping archive codes forward with GEM noon weather, as Pyra does) or overwinter DC (VW 1987 eq 27) would replace it. Noon LST uses a province table (AB UTC-7); how CWFIS treats Alberta's permanent UTC-6 from 2026-11-01 is unverified. Hourly precipitation is applied one hour late in the forecast stream (M12). DMC/DC still fixed within a run. The input-error climatology (`input_errors.py`, May-September noons) was not rerun for the DC fix; noon temperatures ≤ −2.8 °C are rare in that window.
- [ ] **NWP literature gap:** no GEM/HRDPS model paper or Canadian fire-weather forecast verification study is held ([R15] §5); FireSim's own GEM-vs-station error climatology is the only forecast-error evidence.
- [ ] Minor UI: "Click map to set ignition point" hint lingers after a typed ignition (owner note 2026-10-08; status not re-checked).
- [ ] Critical assets (after PR #31): 2 Alberta and 2 ODHF care sites could not be positioned; group homes and sites under 10 units excluded by design; OSM-only care sites flagged "verify".

Closed 2026-10-08: ICS Canada 209 (`d34b349`, `8f349e6`); skipped real-raster tests (`af052e4`); spotting
repeatability (`f681189`); `CLAUDE.md` water and building sources (`45e68d2`); roads
extraction script and `secondary` roads, ODHF care records without coordinates (`2357a02`,
PR #31); EOC manual point, left out of the layer (PR #33, `8da78f5`, merged `dd523f6`). See the decisions log (§3).

Closed 2026-10-09: grid-model point ignitions with off-axis wind (found 2026-10-08; strict xfail
`test_point_ignition_grass_diagonal_wind` now passes): root cause 4-connected labelling of the
starting ellipse plus cross-wind numerical diffusion of axis-by-axis upwinding; fixed in `4bcfb1d`
(PR #35). See the decisions log (§3) and `docs/verification.md` §2.

Closed or adjusted 2026-10-09 (owner delegation, decisions D1-D6 [R6]): structure design-fire
default (150 kW/m², 400 kW/m² scenario) and 30 m neighbour cutoff confirmed; per-building
structure output stays off (aggregate counts only); the footprint check against City
building/parcel data is replaced by the < 40 m² size-filter sensitivity [R7] (−8 % to 0 %
involved buildings; city-wide median nearest separation 2.6 m with or without footprints
< 40 m², so the short gaps are mostly not small sheds; a parcel join stays a possible later
refinement, needed only if per-building output is ever introduced; the earlier measurement on
all 346,238 footprints stands: median nearest separation 2.6 m, 79 % with a neighbour within
5 m, 94 % within 10 m, 5,916 with none within 30 m, median square-equivalent size 11.6 m). Still open: Jasper public-data
search (D5), Qin 0.34 m/s (D6, no author contact), and the grid-dependent front contact found by
[R7] (closed below).

Closed 2026-10-09: **structure front contact depended on the 50 m grid** (found by [R7]: the
all-touched building mask put the nearest burnable cell 0-50 m from a footprint depending on its
position in the cell, so the 10 m test flipped with it; contact 5 / 20 m changed involved
buildings by −71 % to +557 %). Contact is now measured from the building's own grid cells
(`11c1230`, spec §3, decisions log D2 follow-up); contact 5 / 20 m now change nothing, and the
remaining grid dependence (contact at cell resolution) stays open above [R8].
